No, the mobile app can't take payments.

- **Who it's for:** the Expo app is the child app only (`apps/mobile/app` has just `(auth)` and `(child)`). When a parent signs in, they see a "use the web for now" screen, and only parents can pay.
- **No payment code:** there is no Stripe, Razorpay or checkout code under `apps/mobile`. The one related thing is display-only: a ledger row can show "· Paid by card" (`apps/mobile/src/features/history/history-list.tsx:197`), which is the line the child sees after a parent paid by card on the web.
- **Where payments do exist:** on the web, in `apps/web/src/lib/payments/`, with Stripe, Razorpay and fake providers. That is a test-mode showcase only (ADR 0013 and 0014). A parent can hand over an allowance or gift by card, or turn on auto charge, and it posts the same ledger rows as cash.
- **Scope:** `REQUIREMENTS.md` says the pilot never moves real money. Real payments and bank connections are out of scope, on web and mobile.

Adding card payments to mobile would need parent screens built first, plus a mobile payment flow such as Stripe React Native or a checkout handled by a web page.

Both scans are back. Here's how wave 3 sizes up.

**Parent app on the phone**
- **Sign-in:** parents use email and password on the web, and the phone can do the same directly against Supabase. The existing `/api/v1` routes already accept a bearer token.
- **Cheap daily jobs:** approving or rejecting requests, the inbox, recording money received, undo and fix are already shared code. Allowance, gifts, cash handover, rules, renaming a child and timezone are plain database calls that only live in web code today; moving them into the shared data package is mostly lifting code.
- **Costly jobs:** each of these needs new server routes, because it needs admin access or secret keys:
  - adding a child, setting a PIN or removing a child
  - invites and the family code
  - all card payments
  - the parent's Jarso chat
  - guide access, and forgetting one of the child's memories
- **Screens to build:** family home, child hub, handover, history, inbox, allowance and rules editors, and settings.

**Push**
- The database already decides when to notify and marks each notice as pushed once; web push uses this today.
- **New work:**
  - install `expo-notifications` and set up Android (FCM) and iOS credentials
  - add a development build profile
  - store device tokens and add an Expo sender next to web push
  - make tapping a notice open the right phone screen
  - send missed pushes hourly instead of once a day
- Push is only useful to parents once the parent app exists, so the two parts belong together.

**My recommendation:** wave 3 covers parent sign-in, the daily jobs, the allowance and rules editors, and push for both children and parents. Adding children, invites, card payments and the parent's Jarso chat stay "use the web" until wave 4, so this wave needs almost no new server routes.

Should the phone app also cover those secret-key jobs this wave (adding a child, invites, card payments, the parent's Jarso chat), or leave them on the web for now?

Payments are in. To recap what exists: card payments on the web are test mode only (Stripe or Razorpay, ADR 0013 and 0014). A parent can hand over an allowance or gift by card, save a card, or turn on auto charge. The provider keys stay on the server, so the phone needs new API routes to start a payment, cancel one and save a card.

There are two ways to take the card on the phone:

- **Open the provider's checkout page in an in-app browser (my pick).** This reuses the current web flow and provider code almost as is, and needs no native SDK. Payment then works the same for both providers. The cost is a browser sheet that looks slightly less native.
- **Use the providers' React Native SDKs** (Stripe's PaymentSheet and Razorpay's checkout). This looks more native, but means two SDKs to wire in, two flows to test, and more provider-specific code in the app.

Either way, the money and ledger rules stay as they are: the same ledger rows as cash, and the child sees only "paid by card".

My guess is that adding a child, invites and the parent's Jarso chat come along in this wave too, since they need the same kind of server-route work. Tell me if they should stay on the web.

For taking the card, is the in-app browser checkout good enough, or do you want the native payment sheets?

Native payment sheets it is. Here's what that means, partly my guesses:

**Payments on the phone (wave 3)**
- **SDKs:** Stripe's React Native SDK (`@stripe/stripe-react-native`, which has an Expo plugin) shows Stripe's PaymentSheet. `react-native-razorpay` shows Razorpay's checkout.
- **Development build:** both SDKs include native code, so they won't run in Expo Go. Push needs a development build anyway, so this adds no extra setup cost.
- **Server changes (my guess):** the web may use Stripe's hosted checkout, which the native sheet can't use. The native sheet needs a PaymentIntent created on the server, plus a Stripe customer and an ephemeral key. Razorpay's native checkout needs an order id from the server, which the web flow probably already creates. I'll confirm both in the code before writing the spec.
- **What stays the same:**
  - test mode only, and each provider switches on only when its test keys are set
  - the ledger rows match cash, the child sees only "paid by card", and the webhooks still confirm each payment
- **Covered:** handing over a due allowance or gift by card, saving a card, turning auto charge on or off, and removing a card.

Is anything wrong or missing there?

Next question: the Expo config has no iOS bundle identifier yet, so is this wave Android only, or should iOS work too? iOS push and payments need an Apple developer account and an APNs key.

Still waiting on the spec review. In the meantime I looked at how the phone app sizes text today.

The phone uses the web's type scale unchanged, and that scale was set for desktop and children. Page titles are 32 px, section titles 24, card titles 20 (the most used heading on the phone, 34 places), the home greeting 40, and big balances 44. On a 360 px phone that crowds the screen, and it's probably the bloat you're seeing.

My suggestion is a tighter phone scale:

| Style | Now | Phone |
|---|---|---|
| Page title | 32 | 22 |
| Section title | 24 | 18 |
| Card title | 20 | 16, bold |
| Body | 16 | 15 for parents, 16 for children (`docs/design.md` says children's reading text never goes below 16) |
| Big balance | 44 | 32 |
| Home greeting | 40 | 26 |

Alongside that: more white space instead of size, one accent per card, and quieter labels for the small print.

Should this cover the existing child screens on the phone too, or only the new parent screens in wave 3?

The wave 3 design is written and reviewed: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-mobile-wave-3-design.md`. Nothing is committed yet.

**Scope:** parents on the phone, native Stripe and Razorpay payment sheets, push for parents and children, children's quiet hours (9pm to 7am family time), and the calmer type scale across the whole phone app. Android only.

**Decisions I made that you should check while reading** (all marked "my call" in the document):
1. **Parent accounts:** parents sign up on the web and only sign in on the phone.
2. **Save card:** there is no separate "save a card" screen. Saving happens when a parent turns on auto charge without a card, which is how the web works.
3. **Card retries:** a 3-D Secure check or a declined card doesn't end a phone payment; the parent can retry in the sheet. Only success, cancel or expiry end it.
4. **Closing the sheet early:** this doesn't cancel the payment. Reopening reuses the same payment. When the 30-minute window expires, the Stripe payment is cancelled too. A Razorpay order can't be cancelled, so a late Razorpay payment follows the existing "unapplied" path, which is fine in test mode.
5. **Push setup:** phone push works even when web-push keys aren't set; each channel is switched on by its own config.
6. **Quiet hours:** they use the time a notice was created, not the time the sweep sends it. A late-night notice for a child is marked as pushed and never sent, so there's no burst at 7am.
7. **Push sweep:** missed pushes go out every 15 minutes instead of once a day.
8. **Fake provider:** it gets a plain confirm dialog on dev builds, so phone tests run without Stripe keys.
9. **Invitations:** a parent joins by family code or a `jarso://` link. Opening the app from an ordinary web link (Android App Links) is left out this wave.
10. **Device tokens:** they go in their own table, so the web-push table is untouched.
11. **Type scale:** the phone gets its own scale in `packages/brand/tokens.ts`. Raw Tailwind text sizes in the phone app are banned by a test. Titles and money stop growing with the system font size past 1.3×.

The reviewer also found three wrong claims about the existing code. One matters: Stripe's current webhook mapping would have failed every phone payment that needs a 3-D Secure check. That is now covered by decision 3.

Do you approve the design so I can write the build plan, or do you want phone mockups of the new type scale first?

I've closed the mockups. Phones B and C are saved in the spec as the target design, at `docs/superpowers/specs/mockups/2026-11-03-mobile-wave-3-type-scale.html`.

Wave 3 is too big for one plan, so I've split it into four. Each plan is being written by its own subagent in the background:

1. **Type scale:** the phone font tokens, a calmer pass over the child screens already built, and child Home redone to match mockup B.
2. **Parent app:** sign-in, routing by role, shared data, the new server routes, every parent screen, and invitations.
3. **Push:** the device-token table, the Expo sender, quiet hours, the 15-minute sweep, the development build, and FCM.
4. **Payments:** the native Stripe and Razorpay sheets, webhook rules, retry and expiry handling, and the server routes.

I fixed the routes and shared APIs between the plans up front so they line up. That includes the screen paths, placeholder components for card payments, a hook that runs just before sign-out, and the development build that payments relies on. Suggested build order is 1 → 2 → 3 → 4. Nothing is committed.

I'll check the four plans against each other when they're done, then hand them to you for review.

Build them in that order. Plans 2 and 3 had both written the code that routes a tapped notice to a phone screen, so I edited plan 3 to reuse plan 2's version.

The plans correct the spec in a few places; the spec's API route table is now out of date, and the plans are the source of truth:
- **Routes dropped:** sending a note to a child, guide access, and forgetting a memory are covered by database permissions, so the phone calls them directly.
- **Invitation routes changed:** the routes are "look up" and "accept". `create_invitation` turned out to be the child's sign-up request, not a parent action.
- **One route added:** the family home summary uses Claude, so it gets its own route.
- **Fewer migrations:** plan 2 needs no database migration. Plan 3 adds a table for Expo's push receipts. Plan 4 adds a single `in_app` flag and doesn't store payment secrets.

Plan 2 leaves out "Match their saving" on the phone, so that one parent feature stays on the web. Tell me if you want it in.

**How to build:** I recommend a fresh subagent per task with a review after each. Across 41 tasks the plans pass shared APIs between each other, and the payments and sign-in work is costly to get wrong. The cheaper option is for me to build everything in this session, with one review at the end.

1. Do the plans capture what you want, and which build approach should I use?
2. Quiet hours: should children's pushes be held until 7am with one summary push (my pick), or should night-time notices only appear in News?

Your machine is the bottleneck, so I planned the parallel build around it.

**Machine**
- **CPU:** Ryzen 5 5600H, 6 cores / 12 threads. Load is about 2, so there's CPU headroom.
- **RAM:** 13 GB total, only about 5.4 GB free, and 4 GB of swap is already in use. This is the main limit.
- **Disk:** 97% full, with 16 GB free. `~/.cache` is 42 GB and the pnpm store 17 GB, so a prune would recover a lot.
- **Android:** there is no Android SDK, `adb`, emulator, Maestro or EAS CLI. KVM is available, but an SDK plus an emulator image needs about 10 to 15 GB, which the disk can't spare.
- **Toolchain:** Node 24, pnpm 10.33 and Java 22 are fine.

**What that means**
- **At most 3 build lanes at once.** Each lane gets its own git worktree; worktrees share the pnpm store, so each costs well under 1 GB of disk.
- **Lighter checks per lane:** `TURBO_CONCURRENCY=2` and Vitest with `--maxWorkers=2`.
- **One build at a time:** only one lane runs `pnpm build` or e2e at once, since a Next.js build takes 3 to 4 GB of RAM.
- **No local Android build:** use EAS cloud development builds and a real phone over USB. That only needs `adb` (about 30 MB) and the Maestro CLI. The on-device checks at the end of each plan wait until the code is done.

**Lanes**

| Phase | Lane A (phone UI) | Lane B (push) | Lane C (payments server) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Plan 1, tasks 1–8 (type scale) | Plan 2, tasks 1–2 (shared data, parent routes), then plan 3, tasks 1–9 (dev build, database, sender, quiet hours, token routes) | Plan 4, tasks 1–7 (contract, Stripe, Razorpay, service, routes) |
| 2 | Plan 2, tasks 3–7 (parent screens) | continues plan 3 | idle, or reviews |
| 3 | Plan 2, task 8 | Plan 3, tasks 11–13 (phone push) | Plan 4, tasks 8–12 (native sheets) |

Merge order is 1 → 2 → 3 → 4, one PR per plan, rebasing as each lands. Plans 3 and 4 both add migrations, so the database types get regenerated after each merge. Every `supabase db push` still waits for your OK.

What I need before starting:
1. **Commits on lane branches.** Parallel lanes have to commit on their own branches to merge, but my standing rule here is to wait for your go-ahead before any commit. Is that OK? The commit messages follow your rules, and nothing gets pushed without asking.
2. **Disk.** Should I clean up first, with `pnpm store prune` plus clearing `~/.cache`? I'll show you what would be removed before deleting anything.
3. **Quiet hours.** Should children's pushes be held until 7am with one summary push (my pick), or should night-time notices only appear in News?
4. **CI fix.** Should I commit it and open a PR?

Each person controls their own phone, and a parent doesn't set what buzzes a child's phone. Here's how I'd build it:

**Notification settings (per phone)**
- **Where:** a Notifications page in the parent's Settings and the child's Me screen.
- **Switches:** four groups, each on or off:
  - **Requests and decisions:** requests, approvals and refusals, going over a limit.
  - **Allowance and money:** allowance due, handover reminders, card charge failed, money received, entry fixed, jar moves.
  - **Learning and Jarso:** the weekly guide notice.
  - **Notes:** notes from a parent.
- **Pause all:** a "Pause all" switch at the top.
- **Inbox unchanged:** a switched-off group still lands in the inbox and News; it just doesn't buzz.
- **Per phone:** settings belong to the phone. Two phones on one account can differ, and signing out clears them with the phone's push registration.
- **Quiet hours:** children's quiet hours stay on and can't be switched off. The 7am summary only counts notices that this phone has switched on.
- **Phone permission off:** if notifications are off in Android settings, the page says so and offers a button that opens them.
- **Web push:** browsers keep their existing on/off toggle, unchanged. *(my call)*

**How it's built (my calls)**
- The phone push-token table gets two fields, muted groups and paused, plus one database function to save them.
- The group of each notice kind lives in shared core code.
- The push sender skips a phone when that notice's group is muted or the phone is paused.

This adds about two tasks at the end of the push plan, after Lane B's current work, so nothing running now changes.

Should I add it to the spec and the push plan as described?

I'd hold the real push until the PRs' CI has run the database tests, for three reasons:

Children's pushes would start waiting until 7am right away, but the summary push only arrives with the new code. Until that deploys, held pushes go out one by one at 7am. This is harmless but sloppy.
- **The new sweep would hit an old route.** The new 15-minute sweep calls `/api/cron/push` with POST, and the deployed route may only accept GET. It would fail quietly until the new code deploys.

The parent sign-in now has the same "Remember me on this phone" switch as the child's, and the clipped sign-up link is fixed. The changes are on the #98 branch. They're not committed yet.

**Remember me (parent):**
- It's off by default.
- When it's on and sign-in works, the email and password are saved in the phone's secure keystore. That's the same place and the same protection as the child's saved code, nickname and PIN, but under a separate slot, so a parent and a child signing in on one phone don't overwrite each other.
- Next time, the form fills itself in and the switch shows on.
- Turning the switch off deletes the saved details straight away. A failed sign-in never saves them.

**Clipped link:** the "New to Jarso? Create an account on the web" link sat in a button with a fixed 48 pt height, so its second line was cut off. The button now grows to fit the text and stays at least 48 pt tall, so it's still easy to tap.

The grey gear icon overlapping the Sign in button in your screenshot is the Expo development tools button. It only appears in development and isn't part of the app.

The phone sign-in tests pass (32, including 7 new ones for saving, filling in and forgetting the parent details), and the phone app typechecks. I haven't run the full `pnpm check` yet. The servers are stopped, so this isn't checked on your phone yet.

Should I commit these as two commits (remember-me, then the link fix) and push to #98? I'd run the full check first. If you'd rather see it on the phone first, I'll restart the servers.

A subagent is now fixing the 5 CodeRabbit findings on #101. Two are major:

1. **Sign-out race (Major):** a slow push registration could finish after sign-out and register the phone for the signed-out account again. Sign-out will cancel registration properly, and a token that got through anyway is removed again.
2. **Quick switch changes (Major):** flipping two notification switches quickly sends two saves at once, and the older one can land last. Saves will go one at a time, always ending on your latest choice. On a failure, the page goes back to the last saved state.
3. **Overlapping loads:** when two loads of the Notifications page overlap, an older one can overwrite newer settings. Only the latest load will count.
4. **Test script over plain HTTP:** the Maestro helper script can send the test child's PIN and sign-in token over plain HTTP. It will refuse a plain `http://` address unless it's this machine.
5. **Wrong command in `tasks/todo.md`:** the command for running only the push test is wrong. It gets corrected.

Each fix gets its own commit, with tests written first for 1–3. The subagent then runs `pnpm check`, pushes and replies on each review comment. It won't merge.

The new CodeRabbit finding on #102 is fixed and pushed as `455ac71a`, and I replied on its review comment.

**Finding:** the patterns for Stripe's ephemeral keys (`ek_test_…` / `ek_live_…`) only matched letters and digits. Real keys can contain `_` and `-`, so the session-log redaction stopped at the first one and left the end of the key visible. The secret scanner, both the commit check and gitleaks, had the same gap. If a `_` or `-` came early in the key, nothing caught it at all.

**Fix:** the pattern now allows `_` and `-` in all three places:
- `.claude/hooks/extract-log.py`, reusing its existing secret-character set;
- `.gitleaks.toml`;
- `scripts/check-secrets.sh`.

**Checked:**
- On fake keys with `_` and `-` inside, the redaction now hides the whole key, and running it again changes nothing.
- The commit-check pattern catches the whole key too.
- A short fake key isn't flagged.
- No file in the repo matches the wider pattern.
- The tests that run before a push all passed.
- I couldn't test gitleaks itself because it isn't installed here. CI runs it.

#101 and #102 are both waiting on CI. Merge #101 first, then I'll fix #102's docs conflict.

The #101 CI failure is fixed; the push is running in the background, which you moved there. The cause was an existing race, not the push code.

**What broke:** a live notice toast is worded the moment the notice arrives. If the family's currency hadn't loaded yet, the toast used US dollars ("You moved US$5…") and kept that wording. CI was slow enough to hit it. #101 doesn't touch this code, and it could happen on a real phone too.

**Fix:** the toast now keeps the notice itself and words it on screen. A notice that arrives early switches to the right currency (for example "£5") once it loads. A new test covers this: it failed on the old code and passes now, along with the other 13 news tests. Lint and typecheck are clean.

The commit is `fix(mobile): word a live notice once the currency loads`. I'll confirm when the push lands and CI starts again.

**#102 (Razorpay double charge):** fixed and pushed, with a summary comment posted on the PR (https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/102#issuecomment-[PHONE REDACTED]).
- **Server:** each payment provider now says whether it can really cancel a payment. Razorpay can't, so canceling a Razorpay payment leaves it in progress until it expires. A second try reuses the same order, so there's no second charge.
- **Phone:** "Cancel card payment" no longer shows for Razorpay. Stripe cancel works as before.
- **Checks:** new tests were written first and failed before the fix; `pnpm check` passes. No database change was needed.
- **Left open:**
  - A Razorpay payment that arrives after our 31-minute window still gets the existing "received but not applied" handling, which ADR 0018 already accepts.
  - Switching providers mid-payment can still end a live Razorpay payment. That's deliberate, but it could be tightened the same way.

- The browser tests passed on the three earlier #102 commits.

## Parent capability inventory (web) for sizing a mobile parent app

### 1. Parent routes (`apps/web/src/app/(app)/parent/...`)

**Daily / quick actions**
- `parent/page.tsx`: "Your family" home. Sections: needs-you, open requests (approve/reject), notices, summary, timeline, activity.
- `parent/inbox/page.tsx`: notices inbox, with mark-read.
- `parent/children/[childId]/page.tsx`: child hub. Due allowance list, gifts, allowance card, guide card, learning summary.
- `parent/children/[childId]/handover/[kind]/[id]/page.tsx`: confirm cash handover of an allowance or gift, or mark it not given. Card payment starts here.
- `parent/children/[childId]/history/page.tsx`: diary/ledger history and the "where it went" breakdown (review diary).
- `parent/children/[childId]/gifts/new` and `gifts/[giftId]/edit`: give, change or cancel a gift.
- `parent/jarso/page.tsx`: the parent's own chat with Jarso (Claude) about their children.
- `parent/children/[childId]/guide` and `guide/[conversationId]`: read the child's Jarso chats and memories, forget a memory.
- `parent/children/[childId]/learning/page.tsx`: the child's skill progress (read-only).

**Setup / admin**
- `parent/setup/page.tsx`: create family and complete parent profile.
- `parent/children/new/page.tsx`: add a child (nickname, PIN, consent).
- `parent/allowance/page.tsx`, `children/[childId]/allowance` and `allowance/edit`: create, change, pause, resume or stop an allowance schedule.
- `children/[childId]/rules/page.tsx`: money rules, jar-move approval, overspend.
- `parent/settings/page.tsx`: display name, parent details, family code, timezone, per-child settings (rename, PIN, remove, guide access).
- `parent/payments/razorpay/page.tsx`: save a card, auto-charge.
- Shared: `invite/page.tsx` (accept an invitation), `settings`, `dashboard`.

### 2. Where each mutation's logic lives
Server actions are in `apps/web/src/features/*/server/actions.ts`.

**Already in `packages/data`, so the phone can call it with a user client:**
- Request decisions: `callDecideWithdrawal`, `callDecideDeposit`, `callComplete`, `callCancel` (`packages/data/src/requests/repository.ts`).
- Diary: record money received, undo, correct (`packages/data/src/diary/repository.ts`).
- Goals.
- Notices `markRead` (`packages/data/src/notifications/repository.ts`).

**Web-only code, but plain user-client RPCs.** This can move to `packages/data` with little effort:
- Allowance and gifts (`apps/web/src/features/allowance/server/repository.ts`, 454 lines): `create_allowance`, `change_allowance`, `pause_allowance`, `resume_allowance`, `stop_allowance`, `create_gift`, `update_gift`, `cancel_gift`, `confirm_handover`, `confirm_gift_handover`, `mark_not_given`, `decide_allowance_plan`. `packages/data/src/allowance` only has reads today.
- Rules: `set_money_rule`, `set_jar_moves_approval`, `set_allow_negative_spend` (`apps/web/src/features/rules/server/repository.ts`).
- Family: `create_family`, `rename_child`, `update_family_timezone` (`apps/web/src/features/family/server/repository.ts`).
- Profiles: `complete_parent_profile` and the profile update (`apps/web/src/features/profiles/server/repository.ts`).

**Needs the service-role client or server secrets, so the phone needs an API route:**
- `addChild`: `createChildAuthUser` uses `auth.admin.createUser` (`apps/web/src/features/family/server/child-auth.ts`, `service.ts`).
- `setChildPin` and `removeChild`: `revokeUserSessions` and `deleteUser` on the admin client.
- Invitations and the family code: `create_invitation` and `find_invitation` on the admin client.
- All payments: start or cancel a card payment, save a card, auto-charge, remove a card. The payment tables are service-role-write and the provider keys are secret (`apps/web/src/features/payments/server/service.ts`).
- Guide: the parent's Jarso chat (Claude key, admin client in `apps/web/src/features/guide/server/service.ts`), `setGuideAccess`, `confirmActionDraft`, `forgetMemory`.
- Push: `sendChildNote` and push dispatch use web push secrets and the admin client for rate limiting.

### 3. Auth
- **Web parents:** email and password only. `signInWithPassword` and `signUp`, with an email confirmation link, in `apps/web/src/features/auth/server/actions.ts`. `apps/web/src/app/auth/confirm/route.ts` handles `verifyOtp` and `apps/web/src/app/auth/callback/route.ts` handles `exchangeCodeForSession`. There is no OAuth or magic-link sign-in.
- **Mobile children:** PIN sign-in. `apps/mobile/src/features/auth/sign-in-child.ts` calls `POST /api/v1/auth/child-sign-in`, which returns session tokens. The server mints them through admin `generateLink` and `verifyOtp`. `saved-sign-in.ts` and `session-provider.tsx` keep the session.
- **Mobile "parents" screen** (`apps/mobile/app/(auth)/parents.tsx`): a placeholder ("Wave 1 has no parent sign-in on the phone"). It says "Parents, use Jarso on the web for now" and has a button that opens `WEB_URL`, a Sign out button (only when signed in), and a link back to child sign-in. There is no parent functionality.
- A parent phone app could use Supabase `signInWithPassword` directly from the client. The `/api/v1` routes already accept `Authorization: Bearer` (`apps/web/src/features/auth/server/request-user.ts`).

### 4. Existing `apps/web/src/app/api/v1` routes
- **Used by the phone today** (all child-side): `auth/child-sign-in`, `guide/state`, `guide/turns`, `learning/start`, `notices/dispatch`, `outbox`.
- **Usable by a parent app as-is:** `me` (GET/PATCH, profile). `notices/dispatch` is written for the push after a child's action.
- **Provider callbacks, not for the phone:** `payments/razorpay/return` and `payments/webhooks/[provider]`. Crons live separately under `/api/cron/*`.
- **Missing for a parent app:** routes for add child, set PIN, remove child, family code or invitation, payment start/cancel/save-card, guide access and the parent Jarso chat, and possibly the child note push.

## Notifications in Jarso: what exists and what mobile push still needs

**Short answer:** in-app notices and web push (VAPID) are already built for the web. The mobile app has no native push: no `expo-notifications`, no device-token table, and no Expo push sender. The pieces that decide when to send can be reused as they are: `pushed_at`, the claim function, `noticeText` and the dispatch triggers. What's missing is a second transport.

### 1. The notifications feature
- **Table:** `supabase/migrations/20261029181907_notifications.sql`. Columns are `notifications(family_id, recipient_id, child_id, kind, source_type, source_id, window_key, payload, created_at, read_at)`, plus `pushed_at`, added in `20261031080000_push_subscriptions.sql`.
  - Rows are written only by the internal functions `notify_parents` (one row per approved parent) and `notify_child`, inside the same transaction as the record change.
  - `window_key` is a dedupe key.
  - RLS lets only the recipient select their rows, and only while they are an approved member.
  - Read/unread is the `read_at` column, set through `mark_notifications_read` and counted by `unread_notification_count` (`20261030164347_inbox_reminders.sql`).
- **Kinds** (`packages/core/src/notifications/types.ts`):
  - Requests and decisions: `withdrawal_requested`/`_decided`, `deposit_requested`/`_decided`, `emergency_requested`/`_decided`.
  - Money and allowance: `limit_exceeded`, `allowance_due`, `handover_reminder`, `card_charge_failed`, `money_received`, `entry_corrected`, `jar_moved`.
  - Other: `guide_noticed` (weekly) and `parent_note`.
  - In general, `*_requested` notices go to parents and `*_decided` notices go to the child.
- **Shared code:**
  - `packages/core/src/notifications/`: `text.ts` (`noticeText`), `links.ts`, `group.ts`, `copy.ts`, `schemas.ts`.
  - `packages/data/src/notifications/repository.ts`: `listNotices` (cursor-based), `fetchUnreadCount`, `markRead`, `rowToNotice`.
- **Web UI:** `apps/web/src/features/notifications/`. Components are the inbox list and item, the unread badge, `notice-listener.tsx` (realtime), `push-toggle.tsx` and `send-note-sheet.tsx`.

### 2. Mobile today
- The app is for children only. Parents see a "use the web" screen (`docs/mobile.md`); routes exist only under `apps/mobile/app/(child)`.
- The News tab is `app/(child)/(tabs)/news.tsx`, built from `src/features/news/` (`use-notices.ts`, `news-screen.tsx`).
- `src/features/news/realtime.ts` and `notice-listener.tsx` subscribe to Supabase Realtime `postgres_changes` filtered on `recipient_id=eq.<uid>`. They show a toast on a new notice, drop the channel when the app is in the background and resubscribe in the foreground. Realtime is enabled by `20261031044405_notifications_realtime.sql`.
- After a child's action, `src/lib/notices.ts` sends a fire-and-forget `POST /api/v1/notices/dispatch`, which triggers web push to the parents.

### 3. Existing push infrastructure (web only)
- **ADR 0012** (`docs/adr/0012-web-push.md`) amends ADR 0006 (`docs/adr/0006-notifications-as-derived-records.md`): each notice is pushed at most once, and `pushed_at` is the only delivery state.
- **Database:** `push_subscriptions` stores a web endpoint and its p256dh/auth keys. Writes go only through `push_subscribe`/`push_unsubscribe`; a member keeps at most 10, and an endpoint moves to whichever account subscribes on that device. `claim_unpushed_notifications` claims a batch (`for update skip locked`, last day only) and returns role, currency and nickname. `push_subscriptions_for` looks up a recipient's subscriptions.
- **Sender:** `apps/web/src/features/notifications/server/push.ts` uses the `web-push` library with VAPID keys, `dispatchPush` and `drainPush`, and does nothing if the keys are unset. Server Actions and the outbox schedule it via `pushAfterNotices()` and `after()`.
- **Service worker:** `apps/web/public/sw.js`.
- **Triggers:** `apps/web/src/app/api/v1/notices/dispatch/route.ts` (rate-limited, bearer auth) and the Vercel cron `apps/web/src/app/api/cron/push/route.ts`. In `apps/web/vercel.json` that cron runs once a day at 01:30, so it is slow for hourly allowance reminders.
- **Scheduled database jobs:** pg_cron runs allowance generation, reminders and auto-charge (`20261029130003`, `20261030164347`, `20261031071841`; the last calls the app through pg_net). There is no `supabase/functions` directory.

### 4. Mobile build setup
- `apps/mobile/app.json`: Expo SDK 58, EAS projectId `9516ebca-716e-4327-b26e-06e6db0d5f6c`, owner `pratyush618`, Android package `app.jarso.mobile`. There is no iOS `bundleIdentifier`.
- `app.config.ts` adds `expo-build-properties` (cleartext traffic allowed outside production).
- `eas.json` has `preview` (APK) and `production` (app-bundle) profiles, with no development profile.
- `expo-dev-client`, `expo-notifications` and `expo-device` are not installed.
- ADR 0016 (`docs/adr/0016-mobile-direct-supabase.md`) says a missed push after a mobile action is picked up by the daily cron sweep.

### 5. Gaps
1. Install `expo-notifications` and `expo-device`, add the plugin to `app.json`, and set up FCM credentials (`google-services.json` plus the EAS FCM v1 key). iOS needs a `bundleIdentifier` and an APNs key. Push doesn't work in Expo Go on Android, so a development build profile is likely needed.
2. Store device tokens. This means a new `expo_push_tokens` table (or extending `push_subscriptions` with a `kind` column), with subscribe/unsubscribe functions that keep the same "device moves to the new account" rule.
3. Add an Expo Push API sender (`https://exp.host/--/api/v2/push/send`) inside `dispatchPush`, next to the web-push sender. It should reuse `pushPayload` and `noticeHref`, and handle `DeviceNotRegistered` receipts by deleting the token.
4. On mobile: ask permission, register the token after sign-in, remove it on sign-out, and open the notice's screen on tap.
5. Parents aren't on mobile, so a parent push today can only reach a browser.
6. The daily cron is too slow for allowance reminders. ADR 0012 suggests pg_cron with pg_net calling the push route hourly instead.
7. Notice links are web URLs (`noticeHref`), so they need mapping to mobile routes.

timestamp: 2026-11-03T16:21:02.012Z

Questions, ranked by importance:

1. **When a native payment sheet fails or needs 3-D Secure, does that end the payment or can the parent retry?**
   - Today `stripe/map-event.ts` maps any PaymentIntent that has `metadata.paymentId` to a final `failed`, including on `payment_intent.requires_action` (`authentication_required`).
   - If in-app PaymentIntents reuse that mapping as the spec suggests, every 3DS card fails mid-sheet. In INR almost every card needs 3DS. A declined first try in PaymentSheet would also end a payment the sheet still lets you retry.
   - Razorpay pay-now already ignores failed attempts so the parent can try again.
   - The answer decides whether in-app intents need their own metadata marker and their own mapping rules, and what the polled `GET /payments/:id` can show.

2. **How do pending and expiry work for an in-app payment?**
   - Hosted flows expire at the provider: the Checkout Session `expires_at` and the link expiry match `CHECKOUT_MINUTES`.
   - A PaymentIntent or Razorpay order has no provider-side expiry, so it can still succeed after Jarso marks it expired. The money is then taken but marked "unapplied".
   - `reuseLive` only reuses a live payment that has a `redirectUrl`. Anything else live throws CONFLICT, so a parent who closes the sheet early is locked out for 31 minutes.
   - Needed: does closing the sheet call cancel? Does a reopen reuse the same PaymentIntent or order? Does expiry cancel the PaymentIntent at Stripe? Razorpay orders can't be cancelled at all.
   - "Stale sweep unchanged" doesn't hold for this flow.

3. **Is saving a card standalone or tied to auto charge?**
   - On the web there is no standalone save-card flow. `setAutoCharge` opens a setup with an `arm` target (schedule or gift) when no card is saved, and finishing the setup arms auto charge.
   - The spec splits this into `POST /payments/cards/in-app-setup` and `PATCH /payments/auto-charge`.
   - Needed: should PATCH return the native setup parameters when no card is saved? Does the setup carry `arm`?
   - The answer changes the route contract and the Maestro "save a card" flow.

4. **Should phone push still be gated on the web-push keys?**
   - `dispatchPush` returns before claiming anything unless the VAPID keys are set (`isPushConfigured`).
   - If Expo push must work without VAPID, the gate and the `pushAfterNotices` no-op change.
   - If VAPID turns out to be unset in an environment, notices there are never claimed or pushed.

5. **What should the fake provider do in-app?**
   - The registry includes a `fake` provider whose hosted page is `/dev/payments/fake`, used in dev and e2e.
   - Needed: does it get `createInAppPayment` / `createInAppSetup`, or is it hidden on the phone? This affects Maestro flows that run without Stripe keys.

6. **How does an invitation reach the phone?**
   - The web invite page accepts a link `token`, a `code` or an `id`.
   - The app has only the `jarso://` scheme, with no Android App Links (no `assetlinks.json`, no intent filters).
   - Is code entry enough? If links must open the app, there is web hosting and `app.json` work.

7. **Quiet hours: is the window checked at notice creation or at claim?**
   - The rule says "family-local time" at claim. The Done-when test says "created at 22:00".
   - With a 15-minute sweep, a notice created at 20:58 and claimed at 21:05 comes out differently. This decides whether the SQL compares `created_at` or `now()`.

Factual claims that are wrong or misleading:
- **`noticeHref` "gains parent targets as a target enum"**: `packages/core/src/notifications/links.ts` `noticeTarget` already returns parent targets (`handover`, `child`, `child-history`, `home`). Only a mobile route mapper is missing.
- **Payments screen source `parent/payments/**`**: the only page there is `parent/payments/razorpay/page.tsx` (Razorpay checkout). Save card, auto charge and remove card live in `features/payments/components` (`saved-card-panel`, `auto-charge-toggle`) used from the child hub and allowance screens.
- **Stripe `payment_intent.*` "today mapped for auto charge; extend by paymentId metadata"**: the mapping already matches any PaymentIntent with `metadata.paymentId`, so nothing needs extending, and that is the cause of the problem in question 1.
- **Razorpay "add the in-app payment case"**: correct. `order.paid` and `payment.failed` today only act on `jarsoSetup` / `jarsoCharge` notes. Note that web pay-now deliberately ignores failures.

Checked and correct:
- The RPCs listed for moving live in the named web repositories.
- The bearer routes' target services exist.
- The vault `app_url` / `cron_secret` pattern for pg_cron + pg_net already exists in `20261031072658_payments_notify_expired_auto.sql`.
- The Vercel push cron is daily at 01:30.
- An EAS projectId is set.

- **Database:** two tables, `device_push_tokens` and `device_push_tickets`. Functions: `register_device_push_token(text,text)→uuid`, `unregister_device_push_token(text)→boolean`, `device_push_tokens_for(uuid[])`, `is_child_quiet_hour(timestamptz,text)`. `claim_unpushed_notifications` gains a `quiet` column, which means drop and recreate. `trigger_push_sweep()` runs as pg_cron job `push-sweep` every 15 minutes.
- **Core:** `PushData`, `pushData()`, `pushDataSchema`, `devicePushTokenSchema`, `pushPromptCopy`, and `PushResult.held`.
- **Web:** `sendExpoMessages`, `fetchExpoReceipts`, `checkExpoReceipts`, `isWebPushConfigured`, `isExpoPushConfigured`, env `EXPO_PUSH`, and `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/push/tokens`.
- **Mobile:** `noticeTargetRoute(target, role)` and `useOpenNotice(role)` (for plan 2's parent inbox), plus `features/push` with `PushBridge`.

**Spec gaps and wrong claims:**
1. **Quiet hours swallow most child allowance pushes.** `send_handover_reminders` runs hourly, so a child's `allowance_due` notice is usually created around 00:07 family time. It is then held and never pushed.
2. **Receipt checks need storage.** "Receipts checked on the next sweep" needs somewhere to keep the ticket ids, and the spec has nothing for it. I added `device_push_tickets`.
3. **Column naming.** The spec says `user_id`; the codebase convention is `profile_id`, which I used.
4. **Routes vs ADR 0016.** The token routes break ADR 0016's "/api/v1 only for secrets" rule, since the RPCs are plain user calls. I followed the spec and the routes.
5. **Signing out offline leaves the token buzzing.** The spec does not cover this. As a fallback the plan calls `unregisterForNotificationsAsync`.
6. **Assumed from plan 2:** a session `status` of `"parent"`, and that its parent mutations call `dispatchNotices()`.

Also: ADR 0016 still says the sweep is daily; the plan updates it.

I revised plan 3 for the new rule: a child's night-time pushes now wait until 07:00 and arrive as one summary. Only `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-mobile-wave-3-3-push.md` changed, and Tasks 1–2 are untouched.

- **Goal, Architecture, Global Constraints line 16:** `claim_unpushed_notifications` skips a child's notice while it is 21:00–07:00 in the family's timezone. At 07:00, two or more overnight notices for one child go as one summary push that opens News and leaves every notice unread. A single overnight notice goes as itself. Parents are never held.
- **Review Focus #3:** now the test `a summary counts its notices toward a full batch`.
- **Task 3 (SQL):**
  - **Signature:** the claim becomes `claim_unpushed_notifications(p_limit integer default 50, p_now timestamptz default now())`, replacing the old `(integer)` version. I added `p_now` so pgTAP can set the clock; the dispatcher never passes it.
  - **New column and filter:** it returns `overnight boolean`, and a filter skips a child while their family's clock is in quiet hours.
  - **One-day window:** it stays as is. A notice held from 21:00 is claimed 10–11 hours later, inside the day.
  - **Existing tests:** `push.test.sql` must name the new signature. Its family is on UTC, so its claims should pass a midday `p_now`; otherwise a CI run at night would skip its child rows.
- **Task 3 (pgTAP):**
  - a child notice at 22:00 is not claimed at 22:30 or 06:59, and is claimed at 07:00 with `overnight` true;
  - a parent notice at 22:00 is claimed;
  - a daytime child notice has `overnight` false;
  - the DST edge cases are kept.
- **Task 6 (core):**
  - `PushResult.held` is replaced by `summarised`, and `ClaimedNotice.quiet` by `overnight`.
  - `PushData` now has two kinds, `notice` and `summary`.
  - New `planPushes(batch)` does the grouping per recipient as a pure function in core, with tests.
  - New `summaryPushCopy`: "You have N new notices" / "They came in overnight. Open News to see them."
- **Task 7:** new `expoSummaryMessage`.
- **Task 8:** dispatch now runs through `planPushes`. The web push summary opens `/child/inbox`, and the Expo summary data has kind `summary`. Tests are rewritten for summaries, single overnight notices, parents never summarised, and the batch count.
- **Task 12:** tapping a summary opens the News tab and marks nothing read.
- **ADR 0012 amendment and Task 13's manual check:** reworded to the new rule.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=1f75299c-09d8-4eb3-9d0d-e28f70dad242 agent=a29315e3b3af3c5ea]
timestamp: 2026-11-03T16:33:44.880Z

I wrote the plan at `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-mobile-wave-3-4-payments.md`. It has 12 tasks, from the migration through Maestro flows and docs. Nothing else was modified or committed, and nothing in it has been run yet.

**Produced:**
- **Provider contract:** `InAppSheet` plus `inApp`, `createInAppPayment`, `resumeInAppPayment`, `cancelInAppPayment` and `createInAppSetup` on the provider.
- **Core response types:** `InAppPaymentStart`, `InAppSetupStart`, `PaymentStatusView` and `InAppProviders`, with `isFinalStatus` and the in-app schemas.
- **Server:** `endLivePayment`, plus the seven bearer routes and `@jarso/data/payments` `fetchAutoChargeState`.
- **Phone:** `presentSheet` and `usePaymentStatus`, and the filled `CardPaymentSlot`, `AutoChargeSlot` and a new `SavedCardsSlot`.

**Consumed:**
- Plan 2's empty slot components and its `parent-sign-in.yaml` Maestro flow.
- Plan 3's `development` EAS profile and `expo-dev-client`.
- Plan 1's type tokens.

**Spec gaps or wrong claims:**
1. **Storing sheet params:** the spec says store the client secret and order id on the live payment. The plan instead adds one `payments.in_app` flag and gets the client secret again from Stripe when the sheet reopens. Stripe's ephemeral key has to be created fresh on every open anyway, and every parent of the child can read the payments table.
2. **Cancelling at Stripe:** the contract has no cancel method, so the plan adds `cancelInAppPayment` (and `resumeInAppPayment`).
3. **`setup_intent.succeeded`:** it has to require the `jarsoInApp` marker. Hosted setups also carry `metadata.setupId` and already settle through Checkout.
4. **Publishable key:** no `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` env var exists. The plan adds it as optional; without it, Stripe stays web-only.
5. **`DELETE /payments/cards/:id`:** cards have no id, so `:id` is the provider id.
6. **Fake dialog route:** the fake completion path needs a route the spec doesn't list (`POST /payments/fake/complete`).
7. **Cross-plan (plan 2):** `CardPaymentSlot` has no cash/saved split. The plan adds an optional `split` prop and the server falls back to prefilling the split. Plan 2 also has no slot for removing a card, so this plan mounts `SavedCardsSlot` in plan 2's settings screen.

I fixed a real product bug behind the CI failure. The fix is uncommitted, nothing was pushed, and I could not run the e2e suite locally.

**What failed:** workflow `CI`, job `e2e (2)`, step `playwright test --shard=2/2`. It ran on the push to main for commit 057c212, "feat(mobile): child app wave 2, parity with web (#94)". The hard failure is `apps/web/tests/e2e/guide.spec.ts:141`, "off-topic questions are redirected to money topics". On the first attempt, Jarso's answer "I can only help with money…" never showed up within 10s. On the retry, the "New chat" link did not change the page address within 10s. The other three tests in the log (learning, parent-profile, offline-parent) are flaky sign-up timeouts that passed on retry, and #94 did not cause them.

**Root cause:** `apps/web/src/features/guide/components/jarso-page-thread.tsx:29-30`. The chat thread's identity was rebuilt from the page address every render: `new-${nonce}` for a new chat. "New chat" opens `?new=<nonce>`. After the first answer, the page moves to `?chat=<id>`, which drops the nonce, so the identity changed from `new-<nonce>` to `new-` and React rebuilt the thread empty. A child who used "New chat" lost Jarso's first answer, and the e2e test only passed when it checked before that move. The existing unit test missed it because it always started with no nonce. This was introduced in 07a8d3d.

**Fix:**
- `apps/web/src/features/guide/components/jarso-page-thread.tsx`: the thread now remembers both the chat it started and the identity it had at that moment, and keeps that identity for its own chat.
- `apps/web/tests/features/guide/components/jarso-page-thread.test.tsx`: a new test starts a chat from a "New chat" nonce and checks the answer survives the move.

**Checks:**
- The new test failed before the fix, showing an empty thread.
- After the fix, the guide component tests pass: 9 files, 49 tests.
- `pnpm check` passes: 10/10 tasks, 1370 unit tests.

**Not checked locally:**
- The Playwright e2e suite needs the local Supabase stack and CI.
- The retry symptom (New chat not navigating in 10s) and the sign-up timeouts, where the page was blank, look like the CI server under load. No artifacts were uploaded to confirm it, so I left those alone.

**Worktree:** `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/agent-adeb7ac805a1fc943`, branch `fix/ci-guide-offtopic`, based on origin/main.

  - 6fcac30 feat(api): parent routes for children and invitations
  - 9977531 feat(api): parent guide state, drafts and family summary
  
  **Tests added** (apps/web/tests)
  - New route tests: `app/api/v1/{children, children/[childId], children/[childId]/pin, invitations, invitations/lookup, guide/drafts/confirm, family/summary}/route.test.ts`.
  - New shared helper: `app/api/v1/parent-roles.ts` (fake `assertRole`, `currentRole` and `withNicknameConflict`, plus request helpers).
  - Changed: `guide/state/route.test.ts` now covers parent, child, 401 and 403. `guide/turns/route.test.ts` has a parent bearer test with `entry: "parent"`.
  - Service tests: the addChild rate limit and `withNicknameConflict` (family), `getParentGuideState` (guide).
  - In the route tests, `withNicknameConflict` is a copy of the real one; the real one is tested in the family service tests.
  
  **All audit findings folded in**
  - addChild is limited to 10 an hour per parent (key `add-child:user:${id}`), checked before the auth user is created.
  - Ids from the path and the membership win over the body.
  - lookup returns `{ nickname }` only.
  - family/summary checks `assertRole("parent")` first and accepts only `days` of 7, 30 or 90.
  - drafts/confirm reads `{ draft }`.
  - guide/state branches on `currentRole()`, with no try/catch role detection.
  
  **Deviations from the plan**
  - Every parent route calls `assertRole("parent")` itself before its service, so a child bearer gets 403 and the service is never called.
  - drafts/confirm checks the role for the draft's kind (`assertRole` by `isActionKindFor`) instead of `assertUser()`. This is how "child + parent draft → 403, service not called" holds. The service still checks again.
  - The parent guide-state logic is a new service function, `getParentGuideState()`, so the route stays thin.
  - `confirmActionDraft` is now exported from the guide server barrel.
  - family/summary with no `days` returns 400 rather than defaulting to 7.
  
  **For later tasks**
  - `ParentGuideState` is in `@jarso/core/guide`.
  - `withNicknameConflict` and `getParentGuideState` are exported from the family and guide server barrels.
  
  No open issues.
</agent-message>

**PR #95 review: one point, it was valid, and the fix is on the branch.**

**The review point** (CodeRabbit, Minor, at `jarso-page-thread.tsx`): "Prevent repeated navigation after the first reply." It was correct. `JarsoPageThread` passes a new inline `onChatStarted` on every render. `GuideThread`'s effect depended on `[started, onChatStarted]`. So when the page re-rendered after `router.replace` to `?chat=<id>`, the effect ran again with `started` still set and called `router.replace` a second time.

**Fix:** in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/agent-adeb7ac805a1fc943/apps/web/src/features/guide/components/guide-thread.tsx`, the callback is now wrapped in `useEffectEvent` (stable in React 19.3) and the effect depends only on `[started]`. The page is told once per started chat, whatever the caller passes, and the first-reply navigation still happens.

**Regression test:** in `apps/web/tests/features/guide/components/jarso-page-thread.test.tsx`, the first test now asserts `replace` is called exactly once after the re-render. It failed before the fix ("called 2 times") and passes after.

**Commit — someone else made it, not me:**
```
d4f5f5f fix(guide): notify started chat once per conversation
564c285 fix(guide): keep first answer after New chat
```
When I ran `git commit`, the tree was already clean. `d4f5f5f` (authored as Pratyush Sharma, 22:39) already held exactly my diff and was already on `origin/fix/ci-guide-offtopic`. Another process using this worktree, probably another session, committed and pushed it. My own commit and push did nothing, and I did not amend or rewrite anything. The message has no body and no AI attribution. Worth checking what else is running in this worktree.

**Verification:**
- Lint: 0 errors, 3 warnings; my two files check clean on their own.
- Typecheck: all 7 tasks pass.
- Tests: the first full run had one failure in `jarso-page-thread.test.tsx`, under machine load. I didn't capture which assertion failed, so I can't rule out that it was my new check. [CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=1f75299c-09d8-4eb3-9d0d-e28f70dad242 agent=a54db79d11cdb0343]
timestamp: 2026-11-03T17:25:20.308Z

chars: 3449

I found no blocking issues. I checked all six invariants against the code. Five hold as written; invariant 3 has one gap, listed under "Should fix".

**Blocking**
None.

**Should fix**
1. **A co-parent can still cancel the payer's in-app payment, indirectly.** `apps/web/src/features/payments/server/service.ts:500-517`, `reuseLive`. The payer check at line 502 only runs when the provider and channel are both the same. Say parent A has a pending in-app Stripe payment. If co-parent B starts a hosted checkout (`startCardPayment`, which passes `switchProvider=true`), or starts an in-app payment with another provider, the code reaches `endLivePayment(admin, live, "canceled")` at line 510. That also cancels A's PaymentIntent at Stripe while A's sheet is open. No money moves wrongly, but invariant 3 says a co-parent cannot cancel the payer's in-app payment, and the route `cancelInAppPaymentById` enforces it (line 900). This path does not.
   - Fix: before the switch branch, add `if (live.inApp && live.payerId !== payerId) throw new AppError("CONFLICT", paymentCopy.inProgress);`.
   - Test: co-parent B, hosted Stripe or in-app Razorpay, gets 409.

**Nit**
1. `service.ts:862` `getPaymentStatus` does not check `paymentsEnabled()`, unlike cancel. With payments off it still answers 200 for an old payment. For consistency, add `if (!paymentsEnabled()) throw NOT_FOUND`.
2. `apps/web/src/features/payments/server/fake-events.ts:54` always sends `fake_cs_${ref}`, but the fake in-app adapter sets `fake_pi_${paymentId}` (`apps/web/src/lib/payments/fake/index.ts`). Only the log is affected, since `handlePayment` does not compare refs. Pass the stored ref, or use `fake_pi_` for in-app.
3. Stripe ephemeral keys (`ek_test_…`) and client secrets (`pi_…_secret_…`) are new short-lived credential types. Nothing stores or logs them today, but `scripts/check-secrets.sh`, `.gitleaks.toml` and the REDACTIONS list in `.claude/hooks/extract-log.py` do not cover them. Add patterns as a safety net for logs and fixtures.

**Invariants verified**
1. **Child refused.** All bearer routes call `assertRole("parent")`, which returns FORBIDDEN for any other role. `fake/complete` makes the same check inside `completeFakeInApp` (line 944), after the fake gate. Every input goes through Zod; the ids are UUIDs.
2. **Success only via webhook.** No route sets a payment to succeeded. The fake provider needs `PAYMENTS_ALLOW_FAKE` and `VERCEL_ENV !== "production"` (`registry.ts:57`). It goes through `handleProviderEvent` only after checking `provider === "fake"` and that the caller is the payer, or owns the setup.
3. **Other family's payment.** `findPaymentById` reads with the user client, so GET and cancel answer 404. Cancel also checks the payer, apart from the gap above.
4. **Keys and logging.** Only `pk_test_` and the Razorpay key id reach the phone. Secrets are minted on each open and never stored; the migration comment confirms this. The Razorpay prefill goes only into the response, and errors are scrubbed by `errorDetail`. Logs carry ids only.
5. **Webhook mapping.** All four Stripe marker checks match the spec (`stripe/map-event.ts:120,135,150,159`). Razorpay reads `jarsoPay` from order notes only (`razorpay/map-event.ts:174`).
6. **Admin client.** It is used only after `prepareCardPayment`, `assertRole` or `assertParentOfTarget`.

No credential-shaped strings are in the diff; only `example.com` placeholders and `pk_test_contract`.

- repository.ts: saveDeviceToken(db, {token, platform}) -> rpc register_device_push_token; removeDeviceToken(db, token) -> rpc unregister_device_push_token. 42501 maps through toAppError (existing helper in @/lib).
- service.ts: registerDeviceToken(input) and unregisterDeviceToken(token), each `await assertUser()` then `createClient()` (the bearer request scope's client; see apps/web/src/lib/supabase/request-scope.ts and how withApi({bearer:true}) sets it up in apps/web/src/lib/api).
- Route: POST -> parseBody(request, devicePushTokenSchema) -> json(await registerDeviceToken(body), {status: 201}); DELETE -> parseBody(request, devicePushTokenDeleteSchema) -> json(await unregisterDeviceToken(body.token)); both withApi(handler, {bearer: true}). Any approved member, parent or child. Schemas in packages/core/src/notifications/schemas.ts (token regex ^Expo(nent)?PushToken\[[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,200}\]$, platform literal "android").
- Rules: max 10 tokens per member; a token moves to whichever account registers it (deletes other profile's row); unregister deletes only the caller's row. Tokens never logged.

Keep the report under 300 words: list Blocking findings (with concrete fix) separately from Non-blocking suggestions.


[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=1f75299c-09d8-4eb3-9d0d-e28f70dad242 agent=aa77565c4d8dd3b11]
timestamp: 2026-11-03T17:27:32.740Z
time: 2026-11-03 17:27
chars: 3054

I found no blocking issues. The design matches the web push model (`push_subscribe`/`push_unsubscribe`), and I checked it against the existing migration, `withApi`, `request-scope.ts` and `toAppError`.

**What I checked and found sound**
- **RLS and grants:** RLS is on for both tables. Members can only read their own rows (select only). Writes go only through the definer functions, which set `search_path=''`. The functions are revoked from public and anon. `device_push_tokens_for` is limited to service_role. Only the admin client can touch `device_push_tickets`.
- **Authorization:** registering requires an approved family member, and unregistering only deletes the caller's own row (`auth.uid()`). The DELETE answer only says whether the caller's own row went, so nobody can use it to find out about other accounts' tokens.
- **CSRF:** with no bearer header, `withApi` falls back to cookies and its same-origin check applies. Browsers always send `Origin` on POST and DELETE, so another site's request is refused.
- **Logging:** for unexpected errors, `toAppError` logs only the database error code and message, never Postgres DETAIL (where a rejected row, token included, would show up). The design has no other place that logs the token.
- **Bearer:** a bad header gets a 401 and never falls back to cookies. Service-role and anon tokens are refused by the claims check.

**Non-blocking suggestions**
1. **Token takeover:** anyone who gets hold of another phone's Expo token (a shared phone, a leak) can register it to their own account. Their family's notices would then reach that phone, and a `parent_note` push carries free text (`packages/core/src/notifications/text.ts:181-185`). Tokens are long and random, so they can't be guessed, which makes this the same accepted risk as web push. To limit the damage, the phone should drop or quietly replace any push whose `recipientId` is not the signed-in user. Consider keeping note text out of the visible title and body.
2. **Rate limit:** nothing limits how often registration can be called. It is cheap and capped at 10 rows per member, but adding `hitRateLimit(admin, "push:token:<uid>", "10 minutes", 30)` would match `allowDispatch`.
3. **POST response:** return `{ ok: true }` instead of the row uuid. The phone never needs the internal id.
4. **Body size:** `parseBody` reads the whole body before validating it. Checking `content-length` (for example under 1 KB) first would be cheap protection.
5. **Small bug:** an `Origin: null` header makes `new URL("null")` throw inside `assertSameOrigin`, which returns a 500 instead of a 403 (`apps/web/src/lib/api/index.ts`). Nothing gets through, but the status is wrong.

Files:
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/agent-ac8ba6b092e67dcaf/supabase/migrations/20261103210000_device_push_tokens.sql
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/agent-ac8ba6b092e67dcaf/apps/web/src/lib/api/index.ts
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/agent-ac8ba6b092e67dcaf/apps/web/src/lib/supabase/request-scope.ts

I found no blocking security issues in the Task 2 Interfaces block. Every parent route reaches `assertRole("parent")` (`apps/web/src/features/family/server/dal.ts:55`) or `assertParentOf`. Each admin call runs only after an authz check or a database authz step:
- `findInvitation` and `enforceLimits`: `service.ts:186-192`
- `addChild`: family check at `service.ts:116-121`
- `setChildPin`: `service.ts:312-314`
- `removeChild`: `remove_child` raises JA403 across families before any admin call (`service.ts:330-338`)

**Two items to fold in before Step 4:**
1. **Test spec contradicts the routes.** "child bearer → 403 in every route test" is wrong for `guide/state`, `guide/turns` and `guide/drafts/confirm`, which serve children too.
   - **Fix:** drafts/confirm tests "child bearer + parent-kind draft → 403, service not called" (role set by `do-action.ts:235-236`).
   - **Fix:** guide/state branches on `currentRole()` (`dal.ts:71`): `null` → 401, child → existing path, parent → `assertRole("parent")`. Don't detect role by try/catch.
2. **Body can override the route id.** Build inputs as `setChildPinSchema.parse({ ...body, childId: params.childId })` and `addChild({ ...body, familyId: membership.familyId })`. The planned `{ familyId, ...body }` lets body keys win if a schema ever becomes passthrough. Today Zod strips unknown keys (`packages/core/src/family/schemas.ts:45,88`), so this isn't exploitable now.

**Should-fix:**
- **No limit on child creation:** `addChild` creates an admin auth user on every call (`service.ts:121`) with no rate limit, and a bearer token makes this easy to script. Add a per-parent limit, e.g. `add-child:user:${id}`, 10 per hour, in the service.
- **Summary route order:** `getFamilyDashboard` has no role check of its own (`dashboard/server/service.ts:25`); a child's membership resolves to the family. The route must run `assertRole("parent")` first. Parse `days` with `z.coerce.number().pipe(z.union([z.literal(7), z.literal(30), z.literal(90)]))`.
- **Lookup response:** strip `id` from the `findInvitation` result; return `{ nickname }` only.
- **Drafts:** `confirmActionDraft` trusts drafts sent by the phone, as the web does. That's acceptable, because `formProblem` re-checks the draft and each service authorises its ids (`allowance/server/service.ts:272,337,395`). The body is `{ draft }`, so call `confirmActionDraft(body.draft)`.
- **Low, CSRF:** `withApi` skips the origin check for GET (`lib/api/index.ts:10`), so a cross-site top-level navigation to `/family/summary` with cookies triggers a Claude call. It's capped at 30 per hour (`guide/server/service.ts:526`), so this is low risk.

**Raw errors:** I found no leak. `toAppError` returns a generic message (`packages/core/src/lib/db-error.ts:42`) and `withApi` maps anything else to INTERNAL.

- lookup returns `{ nickname }` only.
- family/summary checks `assertRole("parent")` first and accepts only `days` of 7, 30 or 90.
- drafts/confirm reads `{ draft }`.
- guide/state branches on `currentRole()`, with no try/catch role detection.

**Deviations from the plan**
- Every parent route calls `assertRole("parent")` itself before its service, so a child bearer gets 403 and the service is never called.
- drafts/confirm checks the role for the draft's kind (`assertRole` by `isActionKindFor`) instead of `assertUser()`. This is how "child + parent draft → 403, service not called" holds. The service still checks again.
- The parent guide-state logic is a new service function, `getParentGuideState()`, so the route stays thin.
- `confirmActionDraft` is now exported from the guide server barrel.
- family/summary with no `days` returns 400 rather than defaulting to 7.

**For later tasks**
- `ParentGuideState` is in `@jarso/core/guide`.
- `withNicknameConflict` and `getParentGuideState` are exported from the family and guide server barrels.

No open issues.

**Flows** (`apps/mobile/e2e/`, every selector checked against the source)
- `parent-sign-in.yaml` is the reusable sub-flow. It launches the app, picks "I'm a parent" if the child form shows, fills Email and Password from `${PARENT_EMAIL}` / `${PARENT_PASSWORD}`, taps "Sign in", waits for "Your family" and asserts the "Family" tab. If it finds a child signed in, it clears the app (`clearState`) first.
- `parent-approve-request.yaml`, `parent-cash-handover.yaml`, `parent-allowance.yaml` and `parent-add-child.yaml` cover the other four steps. Random values (reason, amount, nickname, PIN) come from `evalScript`.

**Deviations**
1. The approve flow has the child "Ask for extra" instead of "take out". An ask for extra always waits for a parent; a take-out can be auto-approved by the family's rules or go through the goal predict step.
2. I changed the wave 2 `sign-in.yaml`. It now clears a parent session it finds and switches to the child form if the parent form shows. Without this, `pnpm e2e` runs the whole folder in no fixed order and child flows fail after a parent flow.
3. The add-child flow opens "Add a child" from Settings, not the Children tab. On Android, "Children" is both the tab label and a heading on Family, so tapping it is ambiguous.
4. Nothing was added to `docs/api.md`: the Task 2 rows were already there.
5. I reworded the wave 1 "use the web" row in `tasks/todo.md`.

**Docs updated**
- `docs/mobile.md`, `docs/auth.md`, `docs/architecture.md` §8/§9, `docs/testing.md`, and `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 (profiles, dashboard) as briefed.
- `tasks/todo.md` has a new "Mobile wave 3" section: the parent device checklist (every Screens row except saved card) and the plan 1 Task 8 device checks.

**Checks**
- `TURBO_CONCURRENCY=2 pnpm check` exited 0 (mobile jest: 54 suites, 393 tests).
- The raw text-size grep printed nothing.
- I did not run Step 4 (device), the code-reviewer or security-auditor agents, `pnpm build` or `turbo build`.

**Open issue:** the flows assume the hosted test parent has finished setup, has the test child in their family, and has an allowance due (for the handover flow).

The six findings, in brief:
1. `apps/web/src/features/notifications/server/expo-push.ts` + `push.ts`: Expo sends are sequential 100-message chunks with a 10 s timeout each, so a drain started near its 15 s budget can blow the cron route's 30 s limit. Thread the drain's absolute deadline into `sendExpoMessages`, use remaining time as the per-request timeout, stop starting chunks after the deadline (mark unsent as transport failures).
2. `push.ts`: `subscriptionsFor` failure isn't caught, unlike `deviceTokensFor`; after `claimUnpushed` that loses claimed notices on both transports. Catch, log the error code (not the error), continue with [].
3. `apps/web/src/features/notifications/server/repository.ts` (Major): move the two device-token RPC wrappers into `@jarso/data/notifications` per CLAUDE.md rule 1/18 (data package takes the client as a parameter; web repository re-exports behind `server-only`). Keep tests passing; move/adjust tests to `packages/data/tests` if the pattern there calls for it.
4. `docs/deploy.md`: document the Supabase Vault prerequisite (`app_url`, `cron_secret`) for the `push-sweep` pg_cron job.
5. `docs/mobile.md`: EAS/expo commands must run from `apps/mobile` — use `(cd apps/mobile && …)` for `eas build`, `eas credentials`, `eas env:create`, `npx expo`.
6. Outside diff, `docs/architecture.md` ~line 598, section 8: diagram and prose still say a daily Vercel cron drives the push sweep; it is now pg_cron `push-sweep` every 15 min.

There is one commit per finding, with no AI attribution in any of them:

1. **Expo sends ran past the sweep's time limit**: fixed in **ab92b32** `fix(push): bound Expo sends by the sweep's deadline`.
   - `drainPush` now works out an absolute deadline and passes it through `dispatchPush` and `sendExpo` into `sendExpoMessages`.
   - Each request now waits at most `min(10s, time left)`. Once the deadline has passed, no new chunk starts, and the unsent messages are marked as transport errors.
   - A dispatch that isn't part of the sweep has no deadline, so it behaves as before.
   - Three new tests (written to fail first): the timeout shrinks to the time left, chunks after the deadline become transport errors, and `drainPush` passes the deadline through.
2. **`subscriptionsFor` failure not caught**: fixed in **7abc185** `fix(push): keep phone push when subscription lookup fails`. The failure is now caught the same way as the device-token lookup: it logs `"Could not load web push subscriptions"` with only the error code and carries on with no subscriptions. A test written to fail first checks that phone push still goes out.
3. **Device-token RPCs in the wrong package**: fixed in **dc90361** `refactor(notifications): move device token RPCs to data`.
   - `saveDeviceToken`, `removeDeviceToken` and the 42501 → FORBIDDEN mapping moved into `packages/data/src/notifications/repository.ts`, using `@jarso/core/lib` for `AppError`/`toAppError` (the web `@/lib` re-exports the same class).
   - The web repository still provides them through its existing `export * from "@jarso/data/notifications"`, which sits behind `server-only`.
   - Their tests moved to `packages/data/tests/notifications/repository.test.ts` and use `fakeClient`.
4. **`docs/deploy.md` missing the Vault setup**: fixed in **19e4552** `docs(deploy): document Vault setup for the push sweep`. A new "Turning the push sweep on" section covers `CRON_SECRET`, the Vault secrets `app_url` and `cron_secret`, and how to check it works. The card-payments step now says to skip creating the secrets if they already exist.

Every `eas build`, `eas credentials`, `eas env:create` and `npx expo` command now runs as `(cd apps/mobile && …)`, and the doc says `apps/mobile/dist/` instead of `dist/`.
6. **Stale daily Vercel cron in `docs/architecture.md` section 8**: fixed in **768cc25** `docs(architecture): show pg_cron push sweep in section 8`. The diagram node and the prose now say pg_cron `push-sweep` runs every 15 minutes.

2. `e8b2026a feat(mobile): open the notice screen from a push` (Task 12)
3. `d6b10d40 test(mobile): Maestro flow for push` (Task 13)
4. `1a409974 feat(data): read and save per-phone push settings`

**What it does**
- **Push setup:** `<PushBridge />` is mounted once in the root layout (`app/_layout.tsx`). All the code is in `apps/mobile/src/features/push/`.
- **Settings page:** `/(parent)/notifications` and `/(child)/notifications`, opened from a "Notifications" row in parent Settings and in the child's Me screen. Each role layout registers the page with the title "Notifications".
- **Copy:** every new string is in `noticeGroupCopy` in `packages/core/src/notifications/copy.ts`.
- **Docs:** the Push rule and the Device e2e notes in `docs/mobile.md`, `docs/testing.md`, and a device checklist in `tasks/todo.md`.

**Code review**
I ran the `code-reviewer` agent. It found one blocking issue and five should-fix issues; all are fixed, each with a test I watched fail first:
- **Blocking, sign-out:** sign-out could leave the old account's phone buzzing. It now waits up to 1 s for a registration still in progress, falls back to the stored token, and drops the phone's push registration when no token is known but notifications are allowed.
- **Sign-out with a hanging network:** the token removal now has a 3 s budget before that fallback runs. Sign-out as a whole is capped at 5 s.
- **A tap while signed out** is dropped. Before, it fired after the next person signed in.
- **Opening Home** uses `navigate`, so it no longer stacks a second Home.
- **Cold start:** a tap that launches the app now waits for the navigator, then navigates after the layouts' redirects. The Maestro flow now closes the app before the tap.
- **Repeat opens:** a handled tap is cleared, so a recreated app doesn't open it again.
- **Three of its four nits are also fixed:** the foreground handler is removed on unmount, the tap effect no longer re-runs on every render, and a failed save no longer undoes a newer change.
- **Deferred:** the fourth nit. Taps don't check `actionIdentifier`, which is harmless until notifications get action buttons.

**Skipped**
- **No device run** of `e2e/push.yaml` or of the manual push checks (no Android device). Both are listed under "Wave 3 push — pending device checks" in `tasks/todo.md`.
- No `pnpm build`, Playwright, emulator or Docker. No migration was needed.

**Where I went off the plan**
- **Settings page:** I paused it when told to, then rebuilt it to the approved mockup. The early draft was set aside and its temporary branch deleted.
- **Child's settings page:** the four group switches show labels only, per mockup M. The parent page shows a hint under each.
- **Maestro script:** `scripts/child-asks.js` asks for extra through `create_emergency` instead of the plan's `create_withdrawal`, because an ask for extra always waits for a parent and always notifies, whatever the family's rules. It gets the child's id from `/auth/v1/user`.
- **Running the flows:** `push.yaml` is tagged `push`. Run the other flows with `--exclude-tags push`; I left the shared `e2e` script in `package.json` untouched.
- **Data layer:** the settings reads and saves went into `@jarso/data/notifications` as `fetchDevicePushPrefs` and `saveDevicePushPrefs`, following the CLAUDE.md data rule.
- **Contract name:** the plan's `getRegisteredToken()` is `getRegisteredToken(userId)`. The token is stored per user as `jarso-push-token:<userId>`.
- **Turning push back on:** `registerForPush` takes an extra `force` flag, so "Turn on" can ask again after an earlier "Not now".
- **Page states:** the settings page shows "denied" only when Android permission is actually denied. If the user was never asked, it shows "Turn on", which can ask.
- **Test files and plan contracts:** test files are `register.test.ts`, `taps.test.tsx`, `device-push-prefs.test.ts` and `notification-settings.test.tsx`. | Package | Result |

### Commits (oldest first, on main `63255a0a`)

2. `7da025e2 feat(mobile): payments api client and native sdks`
