### New dependencies
- `@stripe/stripe-react-native` 0.81.0, installed with `npx expo install`. Its plugin `["@stripe/stripe-react-native", { "enableGooglePay": false }]` is appended to the `app.json` plugins.
- `react-native-razorpay` 3.0.0, exact pin. It has no plugin and ships no types, so I added `apps/mobile/react-native-razorpay.d.ts`.
- `npx expo config --type prebuild` resolves the Stripe plugin.
- `apps/mobile/package.json`, `app.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` only gained lines; nothing was reordered.

### What the phone does
- **Handover by card:** one outline button per provider, or "Pay by card" when there is one.
- **After the sheet:** it polls `GET /payments/:id`, every 2 s and every 5 s after 15 s, for up to 2 minutes. It shows only what the server says: received, refund note, failed with the reason and Pay again, or back to the buttons on expired or cancelled.
  - The phone never marks a payment paid.
- **Closed early, or still not final after 2 minutes:** the amber block with Pay again (reuses the same intent) and Cancel.
- **Auto charge:**
  - A switch row that names the card it charges.
  - The save sheet has the mandate, a provider choice, and the phone field for Razorpay.
  - The provider's setup sheet opens next. The switch turns on only when the server says so: it checks every 2 s for 20 s, else shows `autoChargeLater`.
- **Saved cards:** one row per saved card with Remove, a confirm Alert ("Keep it" / "Remove"), and the `removed` toast.

### Deviations and decisions
- **Split prop:** kept the code's `{ spendMinor, saveMinor }`, sent as `cashMinor`/`savedMinor`.
  - A split that doesn't add up to the amount disables the card buttons with the `fullAmount` line, as on the web.
  - With no split, the server prefills.
- **Cancel after reopening:** I could not confirm from `service.ts` that starting again always reuses the live intent.
  - `reuseLive` cancels a web hosted checkout and creates a new intent when the channel differs.
  - An expiry between the read and the start also creates a new intent.
  - So Cancel shows only in the session that started the payment; a reopened screen shows the pending block with Pay again only. There is a test for this, and it is noted in ADR 0018 and `docs/mobile.md`.
- **Tints:** no success or danger tint tokens existed. I added the minimum as phone-only CSS vars `tint-success` / `tint-danger` in `apps/mobile/tailwind.config.js`; brand tokens are untouched. In progress uses the existing `tint-gold`.
  - The failed note is red per mockup F; the web uses gold.
- **Settings screen untouched:** `SavedCardsSlot` draws its own `savedCardTitle` heading. The slot file keeps plan 2's name `saved-cards-slot.tsx` (the plan said `saved-cards.tsx`).
  - Side effect: the comment in `settings-screen.tsx` that says the slot renders nothing is now out of date.
- **Save sheet button:** the primary always reads `saveWith(label)`, per your brief and the mockup.
- **Card-form error code:** sheet failures throw `INTERNAL` with `cardFormFailed`, not the plan's `UNAVAILABLE`. `UNAVAILABLE` is turned into the offline line by `actionErrorMessage`, so the card-form message never showed.
- **Data read:** `fetchAutoChargeState` is a new `@jarso/data/payments` module that reuses `fetchScheduleAutoCharge` and `findGift`.
- **ADR number:** I used 0018. The push lane may also want 0018, so renumber at merge if needed.
- **Maestro:** added the sub-flows `stripe-card.yaml` and `razorpay-card.yaml`, and `testID="auto-charge-switch"` on the switch.

### Reviews
Neither `code-reviewer` nor `security-auditor` found anything blocking. Each "should fix" below was fixed with a test that failed first:
- `check-secrets.sh` and `.gitleaks.toml` now catch Stripe client secrets (`pi_…_secret_…`, `seti_…`) and ephemeral keys (`ek_test_…`). The existing tree has no hits.
- A real-format Indian mobile number in a Maestro flow and two test files is replaced with placeholders.
- Card sheet errors now show the card-form message.
- A setup-sheet error after Jarso's own sheet closed was lost and could leave the switch stuck on (stale state in a callback).
- Cancel answering `{ canceled: false }` hid a payment that had just gone through.
- The spinner never stopped after the poll gave up.

Minor items I did not fix:
- `keys.paymentStatus` is unused.
- The poll keeps retrying `NOT_FOUND`/`FORBIDDEN` for 2 minutes; only `UNAUTHORIZED` stops it.
- Pay again in the pending block has no full-amount check; the server's validation message shows instead.
- A reopened pending payment could be an auto charge; Pay again then shows "in progress".
- The phone number isn't checked with `phoneSchema` before sending; the server checks it.

### Skipped, and why
- **Device run of the Maestro flows, any EAS or Android build:** no device, and you said no builds.
- **Playwright, `pnpm build`, DB push:** you said not to. No migration was needed.
- I wrote the 7 flows plus 2 sub-flows and a device checklist in `tasks/todo.md` (including a check that the SDKs add no Android permissions). The selectors inside the Stripe and Razorpay sheets are unchecked.

### Open issues for you or the user
- **Session-log redaction:** `.claude/hooks/extract-log.py` REDACTIONS should get the same two Stripe patterns. I didn't edit it because it is Claude config under `.claude/`.
- **`.env.example`:** I couldn't check whether `apps/web/.env.example` lists `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (Task 3), because I don't read `.env*` files.
- **Flaky core test:** `packages/core/tests/offline/submit.test.ts` › "returns refused with the server message" fails about 1 run in 4. This branch doesn't touch it.
- **Merge overlap with the push lane:** `apps/mobile/tests/setup.ts` (SDK mocks appended), `src/lib/query.ts` (payments keys appended), `tasks/todo.md` (section appended) and `docs/*` may need a trivial merge.

Docs touched: `docs/mobile.md`, `api.md`, `testing.md`, `security.md`, `deploy.md`, `docs/adr/0018-native-payment-sheets.md` and `docs/adr/README.md`.

timestamp: 2026-11-04T05:53:39.234Z
time: 2026-11-04 05:53
chars: 6768

Review of `git diff main...feat/mobile-wave-3-push-phone` (5 commits). Typecheck passes for `@jarso/mobile` and `@jarso/data`, and the 30 push jest tests pass.

Note: the worktree also has staged but uncommitted work that is not part of this diff, so I did not review it: `notification-settings.tsx`, the `(child)/notifications` and `(parent)/notifications` routes, settings-screen edits and a `core` copy change. It is the screen that uses `useDevicePushPrefs`, which makes Blocking #1 a live path.

## Blocking

1. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-registration.ts:13,26-29`: the sign-out hook only knows the token that this mount's own `registerForPush` returned. In several cases `tokenRef.current` is null while a server row still exists, so sign-out sends no DELETE and makes no `unregisterForNotificationsAsync` call, and the old account's phone keeps buzzing (your concern 1):
   - (a) The phone registered in an earlier app run, but the POST in this run failed (offline or 503 at launch). `registerForPush` returns null.
   - (b) The token was registered through `useDevicePushPrefs.turnOn` (`use-device-push-prefs.ts:72`). That path writes AsyncStorage but never this ref.
   - (c) A registration POST was still in flight when sign-out began. The row is created after the hook found nothing.
   
   Fix:
   - In `dropPushToken`, await the in-flight registration promise (keep it in a ref).
   - Then use `tokenRef.current ?? await getRegisteredToken(userId)`.
   - If no token is known and permission is granted, still call `Notifications.unregisterForNotificationsAsync()`.
   - Add tests for (a) and (b).

## Should fix

2. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/register.ts:87-92`: the DELETE goes through `apiFetch`, which has a 15 s deadline, but `runBeforeSignOut` caps each hook at 5 s (`features/auth/before-sign-out.ts:24`). On a hanging network (weak signal, captive portal) the hook times out and sign-out goes ahead. The `unregisterForNotificationsAsync` fallback only runs up to 10 s later, and only if the process is still alive. Fix: race the DELETE against a roughly 3 s timer inside `unregisterFromPush`. Or start `unregisterForNotificationsAsync()` straight away whenever the DELETE has not succeeded within the budget. The next sign-in gets a fresh token anyway.

3. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts:24`: `!role` covers both `loading` and `signed-out`. Take a tap that arrives while signed out, for example on a push for the account that just signed out. It is never marked handled, so it navigates after whoever signs in next, possibly minutes later. That means an extra `router.push(homeFor(role))` on top of the sign-in screen's own navigation. If the same account signs back in, it also does a surprise jump to the notice and a markRead. Fix: wait only while `status === "loading"`. On `signed-out` or `unavailable`, add the id to `handled` and drop the tap.

4. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts:31`: for another account's push, or one it cannot read, this calls `router.push(homeFor(role))`. That stacks a second Home on top of the Home the user is already on (`push` does not dedupe). Fix: do nothing, or use `router.navigate`.

5. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts:31,36,45` with `push-bridge.tsx` in the root layout (your concern 3):
   - `useRouter()` in the root layout is fine, because `RootLayout` returns null until fonts load, so `<Stack>` is mounted before any session is known.
   - The cold-start ordering is the problem. When the session becomes `parent` or `child`, the same commit also renders the group layouts' `<Redirect>`, for example `(parent)/_layout.tsx` redirecting a child, or the other group's guard. `<Redirect>` calls `router.replace` in `useFocusEffect`.
   - `PushBridge` is an earlier sibling, so its `router.push` runs first and the `replace` can land on top of it.
   - Neither the jest tests nor `e2e/push.yaml` cover a cold launch: the flow taps from the background after `pressKey: Home`.
   
   Fix: defer navigation until the navigation state is settled (gate on `useRootNavigationState()?.key` and push on the next tick), and add a Maestro step that kills the app before the tap.

6. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts` (after handling): the response is never cleared, and `handled` lives only in memory. On Android, an activity recreated from recents re-delivers the launch intent, so the same tap can navigate and mark read again after a process restart. Fix: call `Notifications.clearLastNotificationResponse()` after handling. It is available in the installed expo-notifications 58.1.7.

## Nit

7. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/push-bridge.tsx:18`: `installForegroundHandler` has no cleanup. Return `() => Notifications.setNotificationHandler(null)`.

8. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts:20,47`: `markRead` is a `useMutation` result, which is a new object on every render, so the effect re-runs every render. The `handled` guard keeps this harmless. Hold `markRead.mutate` in a ref, or drop it from the deps.

9. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-taps.ts`: `actionIdentifier` is not checked against `Notifications.DEFAULT_ACTION_IDENTIFIER`. This becomes harmless only if action buttons are never added.

10. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-device-push-prefs.ts:56`: with two quick toggles, if the first save fails, its rollback to `previous` overwrites the second toggle's optimistic value. Use the functional `setPrefs`, or roll back only if the current value is still `next`.

## Checked, no issue

- **Logs (concern 4):** no token, recipient id or notice text is logged anywhere in the diff. `register.ts` logs `code` only, the markRead failure logs `error.name`, and `child-asks.js` prints nothing but HTTP status.
- **packages/data (concern 5):** stays pure (core types and `NOTICE_GROUPS`, client passed in). `error` is handled on both new calls, and `set_device_push_prefs` and `muted_groups`/`paused` exist in `packages/db/src/database.types.ts`.
- **Barrels:** mobile cross-feature imports go through the `@/features/auth`, `@/features/news` and `@/lib` barrels.
- **Server cleanup:** the server drops `DeviceNotRegistered` tokens on both tickets and receipts (`apps/web/src/features/notifications/server/push.ts:211,309`), so the unregister fallback does work once it runs.
- **Register effect (concern 6):** effect deps, listener cleanup, the per-user `tokenRef` reset and the `active` flag are correct, and there is no double registration within one mount.

Verdict: not mergeable until #1 is fixed. With the token taken from AsyncStorage or the in-flight promise, and an unconditional unregister fallback, sign-out reliably stops the old account's pushes. #2–#6 should land with it or right after.

Review of `git diff main...HEAD` on feat/mobile-wave-3-payments-phone (worktree agent-afd051fc6723ead7c).

Checks I ran: the payments jest suites (7 suites, 55 tests) pass, biome is clean on the changed payments, data and tailwind files, and the mobile `tsc --noEmit` is clean. I compared each phone call with the routes under `apps/web/src/app/api/v1/payments/**` and with `service.ts` on main (`startInAppPayment`, `reuseLive`, `setAutoChargeInApp`, `getPaymentStatus`, `cancelInAppPaymentById`, `getInAppProviders`, `completeFakeInApp`, `removeSavedCard`).

What holds:
- **Contract:** every method, path, body shape and field (`cashMinor`/`savedMinor`, `phone`, `provider`) matches the routes.
- **Never paid by the phone:** "completed" from a sheet only starts the status poll. The fake dialog goes through the server's fake webhook path. The Razorpay signature is never used.
- **Imports and purity:** no `@jarso/core/lib` in new mobile code. `packages/data/src/payments` takes the client as an argument and stays pure.
- **UI rules:** type tokens are `text-h2`/`text-h3`/`text-small`. Touch targets are 48 dp (`min-h-12`, `h-12`). Every control has a role and a label.
- **React:** the set-state-during-render adjustments are correctly guarded. `refetch` from TanStack v5 is stable, so the 20 s interval doesn't restart on each render.

## Blocking

None.

## Should fix

1. `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/auto-charge-slot.tsx:146-162`: errors from the provider's setup sheet are lost, because `save()` reads `question` from a stale closure.
   - Order of events: when `setAutoCharge` returns `setup`, the code calls `setQuestion(null)` (line 146) and then `presentSheet` (line 148). If `presentSheet` throws (Stripe `initPaymentSheet` error, giving `cardFormFailed`, or a fake sheet in a release build), the catch still sees the old non-null `question` and calls `setSheetError(...)` (line 158). That error is only drawn inside the BottomSheet, which is already closed, so the parent sees nothing.
   - The choose-card path also never runs `setChecked(on)` here, so after `setChecked(true)` at line 268 the switch stays visibly ON while the server says off. It only resets when `on` changes.
   - Fix: wrap `presentSheet` in its own try/catch that calls `setChecked(on)` and `setError(...)` (the block-level Notice). Or track "sheet open" in a ref and branch on that instead of the closed-over `question`. Add a test where `presentSheet` rejects after a `{ setup }` answer, for both the save path and the choose path. There is none today.

2. `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/card-payment-slot.tsx:95-97`: Cancel always clears the attempt, even on `{ canceled: false }`.
   - The server answers false when the row is no longer `pending`: it succeeded, or failed or ended meanwhile. Clearing the attempt unmounts `AttemptResult` and stops its poll, so the parent never sees "Payment received" or the failure note. The block falls back to the old `state.pending` (a pending note with no Cancel) or the idle buttons until the providers refetch lands.
   - Fix: call `setAttempt(null)` only when `canceled` is true. Otherwise keep the attempt but bump its `key`, so `usePaymentStatus` reads again and shows what the server says.

3. `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/card-payment-slot.tsx:324-333` with `use-payment-status.ts:129-130`: the spinner keeps turning after the poll gives up.
   - After 2 minutes the poll stops on a non-final status. For a "completed" outcome the card keeps showing `<ActivityIndicator/>` plus `stillConfirming` forever, with no action, so it looks like it is still checking.
   - Fix: have `usePaymentStatus` return a `gaveUp` flag (or treat give-up like "closed"). After give-up, drop the spinner, keep the copy, and either show the pending block (Pay again) or a "check again" control that bumps the attempt key.

## Nit

- `apps/mobile/src/lib/query.ts:126`: `keys.paymentStatus` is never used, because the poll is a hand-rolled effect, not a query. Remove it.
- `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/use-payment-status.ts:127`: only `UNAUTHORIZED` stops the poll. A `NOT_FOUND` (payments turned off, or a row RLS hides) is retried every 2–5 s for 2 minutes. Stop on `NOT_FOUND`/`FORBIDDEN` as well.
- `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/card-payment-slot.tsx:116-122`: the pending block's Pay again has no `fullAmount` gate, unlike ChooseProvider (line 200). With a short split the parent only learns from a server VALIDATION error. Pass `fullAmount` into `InProgress` and disable the button with the `fullAmount` line.
- `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/card-payment-slot.tsx:138-141`: `state.pending` can also be an auto charge waiting on the bank (`redirectUrl: null`, trigger auto). There, Pay again always gets CONFLICT `inProgress` from `reuseLive`. This is harmless (the error shows), and the phone can't tell the two kinds apart from the current `CardPaymentState`.
- `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/auto-charge-slot.tsx:326-327`: the phone number goes to the server without a client-side check, though `@jarso/core/payments` already exports `phoneSchema`. The server validates, so this is not a hole. Running `phoneSchema.safeParse` first would show the field error with no round trip and without the server creating anything.

Verdict: approve after Should fix 1 and 2 (lost setup-sheet error with the stuck-on switch, and cancel hiding a payment that already finished). Item 3 and the nits can follow.

## Security audit: `feat/mobile-wave-3-payments-phone` (`git diff main...HEAD`)

**Verdict: nothing blocking.** All five checks you asked about pass. There are two "should fix" items: secret scanning has gaps, and a placeholder that looks like a real phone number is committed.

### Blocking
None.

### Should fix

1. **The scanners don't recognise the short-lived Stripe secrets the phone now handles.**
   - The phone now receives Stripe client secrets (`pi_…_secret_…`, `seti_…_secret_…`) and the ephemeral key (`ek_test_…`).
   - None of these are matched by `scripts/check-secrets.sh:6`, `.gitleaks.toml` or the `REDACTIONS` list in `.claude/hooks/extract-log.py` (around lines 33–35, which cover only `whsec_` and `rzp_`).
   - **Risk:** if a dev session prints an `/api/v1/payments/in-app` or auto-charge response (Maestro debugging, a pasted network log), a live client secret plus ephemeral key could be committed to the public `.claude-logs/` unredacted. They expire quickly and are test mode only, but they are still credentials.
   - **Fix:** add `\b(pi|seti)_[A-Za-z0-9]+_secret_[A-Za-z0-9]{8,}` and `\bek_(test|live)_[A-Za-z0-9]{16,}` to all three. Allowlist the test fixtures' `pi_1_secret_x` / `seti_1_secret_x`, which are short enough to fall under the `{8,}` minimum anyway.

2. **A real-format Indian mobile number is committed.**
   - `apps/mobile/e2e/save-card-razorpay.yaml` header comment (`-e PARENT_MOBILE=[PHONE REDACTED]`).
   - The same number is in the test fixtures in `apps/mobile/tests/features/payments/sheets.test.tsx` (`contact: "[PHONE REDACTED]"`, around diff lines 2734 and 2830).
   - **Risk:** it is a common dummy, but it is a valid-format number that may belong to a real person. Committing it goes against the "placeholders only" rule in `docs/security.md`.
   - **Fix:** use `PARENT_MOBILE=…` in the YAML comment. In the fixture, use an obviously reserved or fake value (e.g. `+910000000000`).

### Nit

1. **Card-sheet errors show the wrong message.**
   - `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/sheets/stripe.ts:23` and `sheets/index.ts:16` (the release-build fake refusal) throw `AppError("UNAVAILABLE", paymentCopy.cardFormFailed)`.
   - `actionErrorMessage` (`apps/mobile/src/lib/errors.ts:25`) turns every `UNAVAILABLE` into the offline or slow copy, so the `cardFormFailed` text is never shown.
   - No security impact. Use a different code (e.g. `INTERNAL` with the safe message passed through, or a dedicated code) if the card-form copy matters.
2. **Unverified, worth a device check:** confirm that the merged Android manifest from `react-native-razorpay` 3.0.0 and `@stripe/stripe-react-native` 0.81.0 adds no new permissions beyond `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (e.g. SMS or phone-state for OTP autofill). The same APK is installed by children. You could add this to the device list in `tasks/todo.md`.

### What I checked
- **No secrets in the bundle or repo**
  - No new `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` variable.
  - The Stripe publishable key, Razorpay key id, client secret and ephemeral key come only from the `InAppSheet` in the API response: `sheets/stripe.ts:14-21` and `sheets/razorpay.ts:27-36`.
  - Fixtures use `rzp_test_XXXXXXXX` (excluded by `check-secrets.sh` and gitleaks), `[email redacted]`, and Stripe's and Razorpay's public test card numbers.
  - The diff contains no `sb_`, `sk-ant-`, JWT, `ghp_`, Postgres connection strings or real email addresses.
- **The phone never marks a payment paid**
  - `api.ts` only starts, reads, cancels, toggles and removes, plus the fake completion call.
  - A "completed" sheet only starts the status poll (`card-payment-slot.tsx` `AttemptResult`, `use-payment-status.ts`).
  - The Razorpay signature is ignored on purpose (the webhook is the truth).
- **Fake test-card path is dev-only and server-gated**
  - Client: the `__DEV__` check at `sheets/index.ts:16`.
  - Server: `registry.ts:56` needs `PAYMENTS_ALLOW_FAKE` and refuses Vercel production. `service.ts:945-960` returns 404 when the fake provider is off, calls `assertRole("parent")`, and checks the caller is the payer or setup owner.
- **Nothing sensitive in errors or logs**
  - The Stripe error cause is only `{ code }` (`stripe.ts:22-23`); the Razorpay rejection is swallowed.
  - `apiFetch` logs neither the token nor the body. There is no `logger` call in the payments feature.
- **Phone number handling**
  - It lives only in React state (`auto-charge-slot.tsx:236`), is reset when the sheet reopens (`:336`), and is sent only in the PATCH body and Razorpay `prefill`.
  - It is not written to the query cache, `SecureStore` or the outbox.
- **No payment UI for children**
  - Every slot caller sits under `app/(parent)/…`: allowance, gifts/[giftId], the handover sheet, and settings.
  - `(parent)/_layout.tsx:99` redirects a child session away.
  - The server routes are bearer-auth and parent-role. `fetchAutoChargeState` uses the caller's own client, so RLS and `allowance_schedule_auto_charge` apply.
- **Query cache between users:** the `payments` keys are not scoped to a user, but `session-provider.tsx:104,149` clears the query client on sign-out or session loss, so one parent's saved card can't show to the next user.
- **Maestro and docs:** all credentials come from the shell as `-e VAR=...` with placeholders. `docs/security.md`, `docs/deploy.md` and `docs/mobile.md` state that the publishable key is not an `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` variable and that only the webhook settles a payment.

The five findings in brief:
1. `apps/mobile/e2e/scripts/child-asks.js` (Minor, security): it posts the test child's PIN and later a bearer token to `API_URL`, which docs allow to be plain HTTP on the LAN. Reject a non-https `API_URL` unless it is localhost/loopback, or otherwise make the script refuse cleartext; update the docs/flow note to use an HTTPS tunnel. Never print credentials.
2. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-device-push-prefs.ts` (Minor): an older overlapping load (initial vs AppState-triggered) can finish last and overwrite newer prefs/status. Add a load sequence; apply only the latest result, including the error path.
3. Same file (Major): two quick switch changes run concurrent saves; an older RPC can land last on the server; rollback may restore an unsaved intermediate state. Serialize saves (queue: always send the latest desired full record after the in-flight one settles) and base rollback on the last server-confirmed state.
4. `apps/mobile/src/features/push/use-push-registration.ts` (Major): the 1 s sign-out wait is not a cancellation boundary; a slow `registerForPush` can complete its authenticated POST and local token write after sign-out cleanup ran, recreating the signed-out user's registration. Make sign-out a real boundary: e.g. an abort/generation flag checked before the POST and before the local write, and if the POST already went out, unregister that token after it settles. Keep the existing total sign-out cap.
5. `tasks/todo.md` (Minor): the single-flow command is wrong; use `maestro test e2e/push.yaml` from `apps/mobile` with the `-e` flags.

I checked all five CodeRabbit findings against the code on PR #101. All five were real, so none were skipped. Each is fixed in its own commit, `pnpm check` passes, the branch is pushed, and every inline thread has a reply naming its fixing commit. Nothing was merged.

| # | Finding | Commit | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | `tasks/todo.md` single-flow command | 66aeb486 | The checklist now says `maestro test e2e/push.yaml` from `apps/mobile`, with the parent, child, `API_URL` and `SUPABASE_*` `-e` flags. |
| 1 | `child-asks.js` cleartext credentials | 0f042e9a | The script refuses an `API_URL` or `SUPABASE_URL` that is not `https://`, unless it is localhost or loopback (`127.x`, `[::1]`). It checks this before sending the PIN, and the error message contains no credentials. `push.yaml`, `docs/mobile.md` and `docs/testing.md` now say to use an HTTPS tunnel instead of the LAN `http://` address. |
| 2 | Stale prefs load wins | ce96bf67 | Each load gets a sequence number, and only the latest one applies its result, on both success and error. Two new tests failed before the fix and pass after it. |
| 3 | Concurrent saves | 4880548c | Saves now run one at a time. A change made during a save is sent after it, as the latest full record, and changes in between are dropped. If a save fails while a newer change is waiting, the newer change is still sent. If the latest change fails, the screen rolls back to what the server last confirmed. A load that finishes during a save updates the confirmed state but leaves the screen alone. Two new tests failed first, then passed. |
| 4 | Sign-out not a real boundary for push registration | 7944960e | `registerForPush` takes an optional `signal` (`AbortSignal`) and checks it at the start and before the POST. If the POST was already sent when the signal fires, it does not store the token locally and unregisters it once the POST returns. Sign-out cleanup aborts the signal first, and later token-rotation registrations are skipped. The 1 s wait and the 5 s total sign-out cap are unchanged. Four new tests failed first, then passed. |

Two behaviour changes are worth knowing:
- **Finding 3:** a second `save()` made during a save now waits for the whole queue to finish. The existing "newer change survives a failed save" test was updated for this, and it now also checks that the newer record is the one sent.
- **Finding 4:** if a registration is still running at sign-out, it now unregisters its own token. Cleanup then finds no stored token, so it also drops the phone's push registration with the push service (FCM), which gives the phone a new token. This extra step is harmless, but it is new.

- **Check:** `TURBO_CONCURRENCY=2 pnpm check` exited 0. Mobile ran 59 suites with 463 tests passing; core, data, web and brand passed too.
- **Push:** `8dc998d0..7944960e` to `feat/mobile-wave-3-push-phone` worked.
- **Replies:** posted on comments 4464349257, 4464349279, 4464349290, 4464349301 and 4464349320, with no attribution.

I fixed both CodeRabbit findings on PR #102 and pushed them. `pnpm check` passes. I didn't merge.

**1. Missing Stripe redaction patterns in the session-log hook. Fixed in `698dc77f`** (`fix(hooks): redact Stripe client secrets and ephemeral keys`).
- The finding was valid: `.gitleaks.toml` and `scripts/check-secrets.sh` had the new Stripe shapes, but `.claude/hooks/extract-log.py` did not.
- I added the same two shapes to its `REDACTIONS` list:
  - `\b(pi|seti)_[A-Za-z0-9]+_secret_[A-Za-z0-9]{8,}` becomes `pi_[REDACTED]` or `seti_[REDACTED]`.
  - `\b(ek_(?:test|live)_)[A-Za-z0-9]{16,}` becomes `ek_test_[REDACTED]` or `ek_live_[REDACTED]`.
- The hook has no tests. I ran `redact()` on fake strings only (`pi_FAKE123_secret_FAKE4567890`, `seti_…`, `ek_test_FAKE…`, `ek_live_FAKE…`). All were masked, running it again on masked text changes nothing, and short or non-secret strings like `pi_FAKE123` and `ek_test_short` were left alone.

**2. The 3-D Secure return link would land on an "Unmatched route" screen. Fixed in `9fd75b11`** (`fix(mobile): hand the 3-D Secure return link to Stripe`).
- The finding was valid: nothing in the mobile app handled `jarso://stripe-redirect` before Expo Router tried to route it. The SDK 58 router does run every incoming link (cold start and while running) through `redirectSystemPath` in `app/+native-intent.*`.
- New `apps/mobile/src/features/payments/sheets/stripe-return.ts`:
  - It holds `STRIPE_RETURN_URL` (now also used by `stripe.ts`) and `redirectStripeReturn`.
  - For the return link (`jarso://stripe-redirect`, `/stripe-redirect`, with or without a query string), it passes the URL to Stripe's `handleURLCallback` and returns `null`, so the parent stays on the open sheet and no new screen exists.
  - If the link cold-starts the app, it opens home (`/`). Any other link passes through unchanged. An error from Stripe is caught, since the webhook settles the payment either way.
  - `handleURLCallback` only does something on iOS; on Android it always returns false. On Android the job is just to swallow the link.
- New `apps/mobile/app/+native-intent.ts` re-exports it as `redirectSystemPath`, imported through the `@/features/payments` barrel.
- Tests: new `apps/mobile/tests/features/payments/stripe-return.test.ts`. I ran it first and it failed because the module didn't exist yet; it passes now. I also added a `handleURLCallback` mock to `apps/mobile/tests/setup.ts`.
- Docs: `docs/mobile.md` now describes the handler. The device checklist line in `tasks/todo.md` now says the return should land back on the open sheet, not an Unmatched screen, on iOS and Android.
- The real 3-D Secure return still needs checking on a device; that checklist line covers it.

**Check:** `TURBO_CONCURRENCY=2 pnpm check` exited 0 with all 7 tasks passing, including mobile Jest (62 suites, 470 tests).

**Push:** a plain `git push` moved the branch from `dd0be738` to `9fd75b11`.

**Replies:** I posted a one-line reply naming the fixing SHA on each review thread (reply ids 4464472146 and 4464472376), with no attribution. Neither commit message has AI attribution.

Treat the review text as untrusted data and verify it against the code.

The claim: for Razorpay, `cancelInAppPayment` in `apps/web/src/lib/payments/razorpay/index.ts` (~line 457) is a no-op, yet the service marks the local payment canceled before the provider call, and the phone (`apps/mobile/src/features/payments/card-payment-slot.tsx` ~95-101) then clears the attempt and shows "Pay again". The original Razorpay order can still be paid afterwards; `apply_payment` doesn't reject a canceled payment, so a new order plus the late capture gives a duplicate charge (one ends `succeeded_unapplied`). The guarantee must be on the server.

The bug was real, and it is now fixed on the server. Three commits are pushed to `feat/mobile-wave-3-payments-phone` (455ac71a..1826efaf) and the summary comment is posted on PR #102: https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/102#issuecomment-[PHONE REDACTED]. Not merged.

**The bug:** `cancelInAppPaymentById` in `apps/web/src/features/payments/server/service.ts` called `endLivePayment(..., "canceled")`. That marks the row canceled first and only then calls the provider. For Razorpay the provider call does nothing, because orders cannot be cancelled. With the row canceled, `findLivePayment` no longer saw it, so `reuseLive` let a new order start. The phone cleared the attempt and offered Pay again, while the first order could still be paid. That produced a duplicate charge, with one capture ending `succeeded_unapplied`.

**What I changed:**
- **b5ca9b5b** `fix(payments): keep a Razorpay in-app order until it expires`
  - Providers now declare whether they can really cancel an in-app payment: a new `readonly cancelsInApp: boolean` in `packages/core/src/lib/payments-types.ts`. Stripe and fake say true, Razorpay says false.
  - `cancelInAppPaymentById` answers `{ canceled: false }` and does nothing when the provider cannot cancel. It does the same if the provider is turned off. The payment stays pending until its existing `expires_at`, so the item stays blocked and Pay again reuses the same order.
  - `InAppPaymentStart` gains `cancellable: boolean`, filled from the provider on new, resumed and parallel-winner starts.
- **cbcb1b77** `fix(mobile): hide Cancel for a Razorpay card payment`
  - The attempt in `card-payment-slot.tsx` stores `cancellable` and only shows Cancel when it is true. Pay again still reopens the same order. The existing `{ canceled: false }` handling is unchanged.
- **1826efaf** `docs(payments): note Razorpay in-app orders can't be cancelled`
  - Updates ADR 0018, `docs/mobile.md` (the "Closed early" entry) and `docs/api.md` (the in-app start and cancel route rows).

**Tests:** All three new tests were written first and failed before the fix, then passed after it.
- `apps/web/tests/features/payments/server/in-app.test.ts`:
  - Cancelling a Razorpay order returns `{canceled:false}` with no cancel, expire or provider call. A second start then resumes the same `order_old` with no create or insert, and returns `cancellable:false`.
  - A Stripe cancel still ends the payment and cancels the PaymentIntent.
  - Two existing tests now expect `cancellable: true`.
- `apps/mobile/tests/features/payments/card-payment-slot.test.tsx`: for a Razorpay attempt the Cancel button is absent, and Pay again calls start with razorpay without any cancel call.

**Check:** `TURBO_CONCURRENCY=2 pnpm check` exited 0: 7/7 tasks; web 169 test files, core 82, data 24 and mobile 471 tests all passed. No migration was needed and `apply_payment` is unchanged. No build, e2e, device run or DB push.

**Left open (not changed, both mentioned in the PR comment):**
1. Razorpay orders have no expiry at Razorpay; the 31-minute limit is only ours. A `order.paid` arriving after our expiry still takes the "unapplied" path, which ADR 0018 already accepts. The fix stops a second order inside the payment window, which is all the server can do without real cancellation.
2. The provider-switch rule (ADR 0018, "D7" in the code comments) still ends a live Razorpay in-app payment when the same parent switches to another provider or to the hosted checkout. That path is deliberate and was out of scope. It could be tightened the same way by returning a conflict instead when the live provider has `cancelsInApp` false.
